Outcome definitions and timing

Immediately after story rating and distractor: vote choice/strength/leaning, favorability, Trump approval, and topic approval as applicable; exact wording/coding incomplete
main dependent variables of interest: 2020 vote choicePDF page 7 · Data and Methods, outcome paragraph

Post-distractor outcomes included vote choice, strength/leaning, favorability, Trump job approval, and five topic-specific approval ratings; exact response options are not fully reported.

Estimand

Average effect of showing the assigned statements on respondent outcomes; plus candidate/message-type comparisons
precision-weighted average effectPDF page 9 · Figure 1 discussion and note

Across all four surveys, Biden-statement effects average 3.90 points (SE 0.76), Trump-statement effects 1.44 (SE 0.48), difference 2.45 (SE 0.90; p=.0066); component regressions are only in excluded SI.

Estimator or statistical model

Covariate-adjusted respondent-level regression after 20-fold elastic-net covariate selection; survey fixed effects when pooled; exact selected model NR
used a 20-fold elastic net regressionPDF page 8 · Analysis; footnote 15

A factor-composite outcome selected prognostic pretreatment covariates by 20-fold elastic net without the treatment indicator; the outcome was then regressed on treatment and selected covariates. Pooled models added survey fixed effects.

pretreatment self-reported ideologyPDF page 6 · Data and Methods, pretreatment measures

The article lists candidate covariates (ideology, scales, favorability, prior vote, generic ballot, economic perceptions, and demographics), but not the model-specific selected set or elastic-net tuning details.

Analysis unit

Respondent
Respondents are those individuals who answered the 2020 presidential vote choice outcome measurePDF page 6 · Table 3 and note

Table 3 reports outcome-completer counts by survey/contrast, not recruited, randomized, exposed, or followed counts; placebo respondents are counted in both columns.

coded as −1, 0, or 1PDF page 9 · Figure 1 discussion and note

used a 20-fold elastic net regressionPDF page 8 · Analysis; footnote 15

Standard-error and inference procedure

Tables report SEs and some p-values/95% CIs; SE type and CI construction NR
p-value: Specific = vaguePDF page 12 · Table 7 and note

Table 7 pools Surveys 1–3 or 1–2 and reports specific/vague coefficients, standard errors, equality-test p-values, N and R-squared; it does not identify experiment-specific effects.

precision-weighted average effectPDF page 9 · Figure 1 discussion and note

Across all four surveys, Biden-statement effects average 3.90 points (SE 0.76), Trump-statement effects 1.44 (SE 0.48), difference 2.45 (SE 0.90; p=.0066); component regressions are only in excluded SI.

NR

Demand effects · 1

The limitations explicitly argues why demand effects do not readily explain the cross-candidate pattern.

We hasten to note several limitationsPDF page 15 · Discussion, limitations paragraph

The discussion limits generalization across elections/candidates and time, warns survey effects may exceed field effects, considers demand effects, notes turnout measurement limits, and avoids claiming that voters literally are Bayesians.

Measurement problems · 1

We hasten to note several limitationsPDF page 15 · Discussion, limitations paragraph

The discussion limits generalization across elections/candidates and time, warns survey effects may exceed field effects, considers demand effects, notes turnout measurement limits, and avoids claiming that voters literally are Bayesians.

Other relevant threats · 1

Single-election setting, timing, competing field messages, strategic responses, and external-field replication are discussed.

We hasten to note several limitationsPDF page 15 · Discussion, limitations paragraph

The discussion limits generalization across elections/candidates and time, warns survey effects may exceed field effects, considers demand effects, notes turnout measurement limits, and avoids claiming that voters literally are Bayesians.

precision-weighted average effectPDF page 9 · Figure 1 discussion and note

Across all four surveys, Biden-statement effects average 3.90 points (SE 0.76), Trump-statement effects 1.44 (SE 0.48), difference 2.45 (SE 0.90; p=.0066); component regressions are only in excluded SI.

Estimator or statistical model

Covariate-adjusted respondent-level regression after 20-fold elastic-net covariate selection; survey fixed effects when pooled; exact selected model NR
used a 20-fold elastic net regressionPDF page 8 · Analysis; footnote 15

A factor-composite outcome selected prognostic pretreatment covariates by 20-fold elastic net without the treatment indicator; the outcome was then regressed on treatment and selected covariates. Pooled models added survey fixed effects.

pretreatment self-reported ideologyPDF page 6 · Data and Methods, pretreatment measures

Demand effects · 1

The limitations explicitly argues why demand effects do not readily explain the cross-candidate pattern.

We hasten to note several limitationsPDF page 15 · Discussion, limitations paragraph

The discussion limits generalization across elections/candidates and time, warns survey effects may exceed field effects, considers demand effects, notes turnout measurement limits, and avoids claiming that voters literally are Bayesians.

Estimator or statistical model

used a 20-fold elastic net regressionPDF page 8 · Analysis; footnote 15

A factor-composite outcome selected prognostic pretreatment covariates by 20-fold elastic net without the treatment indicator; the outcome was then regressed on treatment and selected covariates. Pooled models added survey fixed effects.

6. Estimand: The causal or descriptive quantity being estimated is explicitly defined, whether presented as an estimand, a target parameter, a parameter of interest, or a well-defined causal effect. — 1

All applicable components are fully reported.

Explicit causal or descriptive quantity · 1

The paper defines persuasion as the effect of advertising on candidate choice and labels displayed quantities as average causal effects of showing statements.

precision-weighted average effectPDF page 9 · Figure 1 discussion and note

Across all four surveys, Biden-statement effects average 3.90 points (SE 0.76), Trump-statement effects 1.44 (SE 0.48), difference 2.45 (SE 0.90; p=.0066); component regressions are only in excluded SI.

used a 20-fold elastic net regressionPDF page 8 · Analysis; footnote 15

A factor-composite outcome selected prognostic pretreatment covariates by 20-fold elastic net without the treatment indicator; the outcome was then regressed on treatment and selected covariates. Pooled models added survey fixed effects.

Demand effects · 1

The limitations explicitly argues why demand effects do not readily explain the cross-candidate pattern.

We hasten to note several limitationsPDF page 15 · Discussion, limitations paragraph

The discussion limits generalization across elections/candidates and time, warns survey effects may exceed field effects, considers demand effects, notes turnout measurement limits, and avoids claiming that voters literally are Bayesians.

Demand effects · 1

The limitations explicitly argues why demand effects do not readily explain the cross-candidate pattern.

We hasten to note several limitationsPDF page 15 · Discussion, limitations paragraph

The discussion limits generalization across elections/candidates and time, warns survey effects may exceed field effects, considers demand effects, notes turnout measurement limits, and avoids claiming that voters literally are Bayesians.

used a 20-fold elastic net regressionPDF page 8 · Analysis; footnote 15

A factor-composite outcome selected prognostic pretreatment covariates by 20-fold elastic net without the treatment indicator; the outcome was then regressed on treatment and selected covariates. Pooled models added survey fixed effects.

Demand effects · 1

The limitations explicitly argues why demand effects do not readily explain the cross-candidate pattern.

We hasten to note several limitationsPDF page 15 · Discussion, limitations paragraph

The discussion limits generalization across elections/candidates and time, warns survey effects may exceed field effects, considers demand effects, notes turnout measurement limits, and avoids claiming that voters literally are Bayesians.

used a 20-fold elastic net regressionPDF page 8 · Analysis; footnote 15

Demand effects · 1

The limitations explicitly argues why demand effects do not readily explain the cross-candidate pattern.

We hasten to note several limitationsPDF page 15 · Discussion, limitations paragraph

The discussion limits generalization across elections/candidates and time, warns survey effects may exceed field effects, considers demand effects, notes turnout measurement limits, and avoids claiming that voters literally are Bayesians.
